儒鴻(1476 TT) Trading Buy

營運穩定成長，長期展望正向。 
投資建議：預估儒鴻 2026 年營收為 408.71 億元，YoY+7.59%，
稅後 EPS 24.79 元。2027 年預期在產能稼動維持高檔及客戶訂
單穩定下，營收成長至 424.43 億元，YoY+3.84%，稅 後 EPS 25.52
元。考量：(1)3Q26 毛利率如預期的順利回升，預期未來幾個季
度的毛利率可維持現有水準。(2)持續投入自動化設備更新提高
生產效率，並擴建新產能因應客戶需求，印尼新廠預計 4Q27 投
產，推動長線的成長動能。綜上所述，對儒鴻的投資建議為
Trading Buy，目標價 385 元 (2027 年 PER 15.5X)。  
預估 4Q26 營收維持成長，稅後 EPS 6.09 元：儒鴻 3Q26 營收
維持高檔為 105.72 億元，YoY+9.74%， QoQ+4.82%，毛利率亦
受惠客戶新產品出貨增加，產品組合優化及匯率相對友善等因
素，順利提升至 31.18%，稅後 EPS 為 6.36 元，符合預期。預期
4Q26 由於成衣價格上漲且客戶新品訂單持續出貨，可推動儒鴻
營收維持成長，預估儒鴻 4Q26 營收為 105.93 億元，
YoY+14.34%，QoQ+0.20%，毛利率 31.23%，稅後 EPS 6.09 元。 
美國期中選舉結束，市場不確定因素消除，儒鴻訂單能見度維
持六個月左右：美國期中選舉在民主黨獲勝的結果下，可預期
極端的關稅政策不會再發生，不確定性因素逐漸消除，不過目
前也沒看到品牌端有追加訂單的動作出現，預期未來幾個月的
訂單及終端銷售會處於正常的水準。目前儒鴻的訂單能見度維
持在六個月左右，市場庫存也在相對健康水準，整體市況穩定。 
儒鴻持續投入自動化設備更新提高生產效率，並擴建新產能因
應客戶需求，印尼新廠預計 4Q27 投產：儒鴻持續擴增新產能及
自動化設備更新以因應客戶需求，除了在台灣、越南二地的生
產設備持續更新外，印尼廠新產能也計劃在 4Q27 產能投產。目
前印尼廠的產能利用率維持滿載水準，印尼廠的生產需求未來
的成長空間仍大，預期等印尼廠三期投產後，可為儒鴻帶來下
一段成長的動能。

### Page 2

個股報告

2 群益投顧 
  儒鴻的 ESG：  
儒鴻 2016 年即制定溫室氣體盤查管理程序，並依循「儒鴻企業環安衛及能
源政策」設置溫室氣體盤查推行委員會，由董事長室擔任主任委員，推動廠
區溫室氣體盤查，並以 2017 年作為公司溫室氣體盤查的基準年，推動溫室
氣體減量。此外，致力於用水回收，除了在製程蒸氣冷凝回收使用外，更引
進新技術改善廢水回收。 
公司簡介： 
儒鴻為台灣主要紡織業者，具染整、織布、成衣上下游一貫生產能力，主要
產品為各式成衣，並生產機能布料應用在各類運動休閒服飾上。國際各知名
服飾品牌及通路商如 NIKE、 UA、 LULULEMON 、 TARGET、 COSTCO 皆
為儒鴻的客戶。 
預估 4Q26 營收維持成長，稅後 EPS 6.09 元： 
儒鴻 3Q26 營收維持高檔為 105.72 億元，YoY+9.74%，QoQ+4.82%，毛利率
亦受惠客戶新產品出貨增加，產品組合優化及匯率相對友善等因素，順利提
升至 31.18%，稅後 EPS 為 6.36 元，符合預期。 
預期 4Q26 由於成衣價格上漲且客戶新品訂單持續出貨，可推動儒鴻營收維
持成長，預估儒鴻 4Q26 營收為 105.93 億元，YoY+14.34%， QoQ+0.20%，
毛利率 31.23%，稅後 EPS 6.09 元。 
美國期中選舉結束，市場不確定因素消除，儒鴻訂單能見度維持六個月左
右： 
美國期中選舉在民主黨獲勝的結果下，可預期極端的關稅政策不會再發生，
不確定性因素逐漸消除，不過目前也沒看到品牌端有追加訂單的動作出現，
預期未來幾個月的訂單及終端銷售會處於正常的水準。目前儒鴻的訂單能
見度維持在六個月左右，市場庫存也在相對健康水準，整體市況穩定。 
儒鴻持續投入自動化設備更新提高生產效率，並擴建新產能因應客戶需求，
印尼新廠預計 4Q27 投產： 
儒鴻持續擴增新產能及自動化設備更新以因應客戶需求，除了在台灣、越南
二地的生產設備持續更新外，印尼廠新產能也計劃在 4Q27 產能投產。目前
印尼廠的產能利用率維持滿載水準，印尼廠的生產需求未來的成長空間仍
大，預期等印尼廠三期投產後，可為儒鴻帶來下一段成長的動能。 
投資建議： 
預估儒鴻 2026 年營收為 408.71 億元，YoY+7.59%，稅後 EPS 24.79 元。2027
年預期在產能稼動維持高檔及客戶訂單穩定下，營收成長至 424.43 億元，
YoY+3.84%，稅後 EPS 25.52 元。考量：(1)3Q26 毛利率如預期的順利回升，
預期未來幾個季度的毛利率可維持現有水準。(2)持續投入自動化設備更新
提高生產效率，並擴建新產能因應客戶需求，印尼新廠預計 4Q27 投產，推
動長線的成長動能。綜上所述，對儒鴻的投資建議為 Trading Buy，目標價
385 元 (2027 年 PER 15.5X)。

6 群益投顧 
  【投資評等說明】 
  評等 定義 
  強力買進(Strong Buy)  首次評等潛在上漲空間≥ 35%  
  買進(Buy)  15% ≤首次評等潛在上漲空間<35%  
  區間操作(Trading Buy) 5%≤首次評等潛在上漲空間<15%

中立(Neutral)  
無法由基本面給予投資評等 
預期近期股價將處於盤整 
建議降低持股

【投資評等調整機制】 
  投資評等係「首次」給予特定個股投資評等時，其前一日收盤價相較 3 個月
目標價之潛在上漲空間計算而得。個股投資評等分為四個等級，定義如上。
爾後的投資評等係依循「首次評等」，直到停止推薦。 
「強力買進」、「買進」及「區間操作」均有 upside 目標價。差別僅在於，
首次評等時潛在報酬率不同。「中立」則無目標價。 
一旦我們給予特定個股「強力買進」、「買進」或「區間操作」之投資評等，
就是責任的開始，爾後將透過各式研究報告作定期性、持續性追蹤基本面及
股價變化，直到停止推薦。 
停止推薦情境： 
1. 達目標價。 
2. 雖未達原訂目標價，但檢視基本面、訊息面、籌碼面等多方訊息，研
判股價上漲空間已然有限，將適時出具降評報告。 
3. 推薦後股價不漲反跌，亦將出具降評報告。

【免責聲明】 
  本研究報告僅提供予特定人之客戶作為參考資料「非經同意不得轉載」。我
們並不確保此資訊的完整性與正確性，投資人應了解，報告中有關未來預測
之陳述可能不會實現，因而不應被依賴。而且此報告並非根據特定投資目的
或依預定對象之財務狀況所撰寫出來的，因此，此研究報告的目的，既非對
投資人於買賣證券、選擇權、期貨或其他證券相關之衍生性商品提供詢價服
務，亦非作為進行交易的要約。投資人應注意到相關證券之價值及收益，可
能會有無預警地上升或下降，產生投資回報金額可能比原始投資來得少的
情形。

Document 030: 2026-11-09_［みずほ経済EXPRESS］中国が政策金利を翌日物に変更_65c10ae5

Source PDF: all report pdf/2026-11-09_［みずほ経済EXPRESS］中国が政策金利を翌日物に変更_65c10ae5.pdf
Report source: Unknown
Date: 未知日期

### Page 1

みずほ経済 EXPRESS

中国が政策金利を翌日物に変更 
～銀行金利市場化の長い道のり～

調査部 主任エコノミスト 月岡直樹 
080-1069-6684 naoki.tsukioka@mizuho-rt.co.jp 
■ 人民銀がリバースレポ翌日物の日常オペを開始。短期金利の誘導精度を高める狙い 
中国の中央銀行に当たる中国人民銀行（PBOC）が10月30日、市場に短期流動性を供給する「売り戻
し条件付き債券購入（リバースレポ）」翌日物の公開市場操作（オペ）を11月2日より毎営業日実施す
ると発表した。これまで原則として毎営業日実施していたリバースレポ7日物から切り替える。翌日物
のオペは固定金利、数量入札の方式で行い、その金利を政策金利と位置づける。インターバンク市場
のレポ取引では翌日物が大半を占めていることから、政策金利を7日物から翌日物に変更することで
期間のズレを解消し、短期市場金利の誘導精度を高める狙いがある。新たなオペ初日の金利は1.25％
であった（前営業日のリバースレポ7日物の金利は1.40％）。 
この動きは、PBOCの潘功勝行長（総裁）が10月19日の講演で明らかにしていた金融政策運営の改革
方針を実行に移したものである。潘氏は「銀行間債券担保付レポ翌日物金利（DR001）は事実上、中
央銀行の金融政策が調節する目標金利となっているが、それと同時に、中央銀行の公開市場操作の主
要な種類と主要な政策金利の期限はなお7日で、両者は完全には一致しない」ことを認めた上で、「適
切なタイミングで公開市場の主要な操作種類の期限を翌日物に切り替え、公開市場の翌日物レポ金利
を主要な政策金利とすることを検討している」と予告していた。この切り替えによって、PBOCの金融
政策運営は米連邦準備制度理事会（FRB）や日本銀行のそれに近づくことになる。 
さらに、潘氏は銀行貸出金利の改革を進めていることも明らかにした。最優遇貸出金利（LPR）を
参照して貸出金利を決定するこれまでの運用に加え、DR001などの短期市場金利を直接参照して金利
を決定する方法を試験的に導入したのである。今後、財務力のある企業は商業銀行と協議の上でDR001
もしくはLPRを参照した貸出金利を選択することができ、小規模企業や個人には引き続きLPRを参照
した金利を適用するとしている。経済誌『財新』によれば、2026年7月以降にDR001を参照した銀行貸
出がすでに全国で100件以上実施されているという。 
PBOCが新たな銀行貸出金利の決定メカニズムを模索する背景には、LPRが「最優遇」から乖離し、
貸出金利の実勢を十分に反映できていないことがあるとみられる。これは、2013年7月に下限規制が撤
廃される前の貸出基準金利が基準の70％まで引き下げを許容していた歴史的経緯から、信用力の高い
顧客にLPR以下の金利で貸出を実行する慣行が残っているためでもある。潘氏は改革を進めると同時
に、すでに実務的な役割を終えている貸出基準金利と預金基準金利を正式に廃止するとした。 
2026 年 11 月 9 日

### Page 2

2

■ 貸出基準金利から最優遇貸出金利へ移行。ただ、実勢金利は「最優遇」から乖離 
PBOCは2010年代以降、銀行金利の市場化改革を慎重に推し進めてきた（図表1）。改革以前はPBOC
が貸出基準金利と預金基準金利を直接コントロールし、商業銀行がその上下一定の範囲内で貸出・預
金金利を決定する制度となっていたが、PBOCは2013年7月に貸出金利の下限規制を撤廃し、同年10月
には全国銀行間コール市場センター（全国銀行間同業拆借中心。以下「コール市場センター」）を通
じて貸出基礎金利（旧LPR）の発表を開始した。旧LPRは、主要9行（のち10行）の報告銀行がコール
市場センターに貸出の最優遇金利を報告し、センター側がそのうち最高値と最低値を除いた報告金利
を報告銀行の貸出残高で加重平均して算出するものであった。 
PBOCは銀行貸出金利が市場原理に基づき決定されるメカニズムへの移行を意図していたが、PBOC
が引き続き基準金利を操作して利下げを行い、報告銀行も基準金利を強く意識して最優遇金利を報告
したため、旧LPRは貸出基準金利に張り付いて変動した。PBOCは「銀行が貸出を実行する際、多くが
なお貸出基準金利を参照して金利を決定し、特に一部の銀行が協調行動を通じて貸出基準金利の一定
図表1 銀行金利の市場化改革 
年月 No. 改革内容 
2004/10  銀行貸出金利の上限規制と銀行預金金利の下限規制を撤廃 
2013/7  ① 銀行貸出金利の下限規制を撤廃（撤廃前の下限は貸出基準金利の 70％） 
2013/10 ② 貸出基礎金利（旧 LPR）の発表を開始 
2015/10 ③ 銀行預金金利の上限規制を撤廃（撤廃前の上限は預金基準金利の 150％） 
2019/8 ④ 貸出市場報告金利（新 LPR）の発表を開始。中期貸出ファシリティ（MLF）金利を参照金利に採用 
2024/7 [1] レポおよびリバースレポ翌日物の臨時オペを開始（金利はリバースレポ 7 日物の▲0.2％と＋0.5％） 
 ⑤ リバースレポ 7 日物金利を LPR の参照金利に位置づけ 
2026/6 [2] レポおよびリバースレポ翌日物の臨時オペを変更（金利はリバースレポ 7 日物の±0.25％） 
2026/11 ⑥ リバースレポ翌日物の日常的なオペを開始。その金利を政策金利に位置づけ

### Page 3

3

倍率（例えば0.9倍）を隠れた下限金利として設定しており、市場金利が実体経済に伝達することを妨
げている」と指摘している。 
そこで、PBOCは2019年8月、貸出市場報告金利（新LPR）と呼ばれる新たなメカニズムを導入する。
中長期流動性の供給ツールである中期貸出ファシリティ（MLF）の金利を参照し、銀行がこれに資金
調達コストや市場需給、リスクプレミアムを反映した最優遇金利をコール市場センターに報告する方
法である。報告銀行は中小銀行を追加して18行（現在20行）まで拡大し、算出方法も加重平均から単
純平均に変更したことで、算出結果がウェイトの高い大手行の報告金利に引っ張られる傾向を排し、
指標としての包括性も高まった。この改革により、LPRは政策金利であるMLF1年物の金利と連動する
ようになったが、LPRが「最優遇」金利から乖離した状態は依然として解消されなかった。報告銀行
が旧LPRの時と同様にMLF金利を強く意識して金利を報告したためと考えられる。 
図表3は新LPRと実際の銀行貸出金利との関係を見たものである。LPRを下回る金利での貸出比率が
年々高まっていることが分かる。LPR1年物は2019年8月～2026年10月までの間に計▲1.25％Pt引き下げ
られているが、実勢金利は資金需要の低迷もあってそれ以上に下がっていることを示唆している。 
一方、銀行預金金利は2015年に上限規制を撤廃した後も、銀行間の過度な預金獲得競争を防ぐため
窓口指導や業界の自主規制の形をとった上限管理を維持し、2022年4月になって10年物国債利回りや
LPR1年物を参照して金利を決定するメカニズムを導入した。上限管理が廃止されたわけではないが、
預金金利は近年、市場金利の低下に伴って低下傾向にある。 
■ 市場化改革の成果を銀行の実際の金利設定行動に定着させられるかが今後の焦点に 
PBOCは、2019年頃までに政策金利をリバースレポ7日物金利（短期）と MLF（中長期）の 2本立てと
する金融政策運営の枠組みを整え、新LPRの導入でその枠組みを明確化した（月岡（2024）参照）。
2024年7月には、政策金利をリバースレポ7日物に一本化し、LPRの参照金利もMLFからこれに移行さ
せた。中長期金利を市場の決定に委ねる形へと歩を進めたのである。 
PBOCは同時に、誘導目標とする短期金利を一定の範囲内で安定させるため金利コリドー（回廊）の

4

仕組みも整える（図表4）。2024年7月、流動性を吸収するレポと供給するリバースレポの翌日物の臨
時オペを開始し、レポ金利をリバースレポ7日物の▲0.2％Pt、リバースレポ金利を7日物の＋0.5％Ptに
設定することでコリドーの幅を狭めた。さらに、2026年6月には臨時オペの金利をそれぞれ▲0.25％Pt、
＋0.25％Ptに調整し、誘導目標とするDR001が臨時オペ金利の上下限を継続的に逸脱した場合にオペを
実施すると明確化した。リバースレポ翌日物の日常的なオペを実施する準備を整えた形である。 
このように、PBOCは政策金利による短期市場金利の誘導精度を高めるとともに、市場金利の変化を
実際の銀行貸出金利へと円滑に波及させるメカニズムの構築を進めてきた。今回、政策金利をリバー
スレポ翌日物金利に変更し、LPRを介さずにDR001を直接参照する貸出金利の決定方式を導入したこ
とは、一連の市場化改革における重要な節目と位置づけることができる。DR001参照型の金利は指標
金利としてのLPRを代替するものではなく、PBOCは両者を併存させながら金利波及経路を改善するア
プローチを続けるとみられる。今後は、市場化改革の成果を商業銀行の実際の金利設定行動に定着さ
せられるかが焦点となろう。

四、前十个月人民币存款增加21.97万亿元
10月末，本外币存款余额358.81万亿元，同比增长7.8%。月末人民币存款余额350.65万亿元，同比增长7.7%。前十个月人民币存款增加21.97万亿元。其中，住户存款增加8.47万亿元，非金融企业存款增加1.91万亿元，财政性存款增加3.03万亿元，非银行业金融机构存款
增加5.99万亿元。10月末，外币存款余额1.22万亿美元，同比增长16.9%。前十个月外币存款增加1553亿美元。
五、前十个月人民币贷款增加11.42万亿元
10月末，本外币贷款余额287.25万亿元，同比增长4.6%。月末人民币贷款余额283.33万亿元，同比增长4.7%。前十个月人民币贷款增加11.42万亿元。分部门看，住户贷款减少1.46万亿元，其中，短期贷款减少1.41万亿元，中长期贷款减少501亿元；企（事）业单位贷款
增加12.39万亿元，其中，短期贷款增加4.65万亿元，中长期贷款增加6.69万亿元，票据融资增加9283亿元；非银行业金融机构贷款减少2395亿元。10月末，外币贷款余额5831亿美元，同比增长5.2%。前十个月外币贷款增加381亿美元。
六、10月份银行间人民币市场同业拆借月加权平均利率为1.3%，质押式债券回购月加权平均利率为1.33%
10月份银行间人民币市场以拆借、现券和回购方式合计成交167.98万亿元，日均成交9.33万亿元，日均成交同比增长1.9%。其中，同业拆借日均成交同比下降10.8%，现券日均成交同比增长4.7%，质押式回购日均成交同比增长1.3%。10月份同业拆借加权平均利率为1.3%，
比上月低0.1个百分点，比上年同期低0.09个百分点；质押式回购加权平均利率为1.33%，比上月低0.07个百分点，比上年同期低0.07个百分点。
七、10月份经常项下跨境人民币结算金额为1.9万亿元，直接投资跨境人民币结算金额为0.56万亿元
10月份，经常项下跨境人民币结算金额为1.9万亿元，其中货物贸易、服务贸易及其他经常项目分别为1.43万亿元、0.47万亿元；直接投资跨境人民币结算金额为0.56万亿元，其中对外直接投资、外商直接投资分别为0.21万亿元、0.35万亿元。

2026年10月金融数据

2026年11月13日，人民银行发布2026年1-10月金融统计数据报告。数据显示，2026年前十个月社会融资规模增量累计为28.61万亿元，比上年同期少2.29万亿元。10月末，广义货币(M2)余额359.06万亿元，同比增长7.1%。狭义货币(M1)余额116.9万亿元，同比增长
4.4%。流通中货币(M0)余额15万亿元，同比增长10.7%。前十个月净投放现金9023亿元。
一、社会融资规模存量同比增长7.2%
初步统计，2026年10月末社会融资规模存量为469.16万亿元，同比增长7.2%。其中，对实体经济发放的人民币贷款余额279.31万亿元，同比增长4.6%；对实体经济发放的外币贷款折合人民币余额1.26万亿元，同比增长9%；委托贷款余额11.53万亿元，同比增长1.7%；信托
贷款余额4.49万亿元，同比下降0.6%；未贴现的银行承兑汇票余额2.05万亿元，同比下降4.4%；企业债券余额36.92万亿元，同比增长9.6%；政府债券余额106.41万亿元，同比增长14.4%；非金融企业境内股票余额12.77万亿元，同比增长5.5%。
二、前十个月社会融资规模增量累计为28.61万亿元
初步统计，2026年前十个月社会融资规模增量累计为28.61万亿元，比上年同期少2.29万亿元。其中，对实体经济发放的人民币贷款增加10.91万亿元，同比少增3.61万亿元；对实体经济发放的外币贷款折合人民币增加2546亿元，同比多增3692亿元；委托贷款增加2084亿
元，同比多增1002亿元；信托贷款减少1798亿元，同比多减3958亿元；未贴现的银行承兑汇票减少939亿元，同比多减1055亿元；企业债券净融资3.1万亿元，同比多1.27万亿元；政府债券净融资11.49万亿元，同比少4592亿元；非金融企业境内股票融资5705亿元，同比多
1842亿元。
三、广义货币增长7.1%
10月末，广义货币(M2)余额359.06万亿元，同比增长7.1%。狭义货币(M1)余额116.9万亿元，同比增长4.4%。流通中货币(M0)余额15万亿元，同比增长10.7%。前十个月净投放现金9023亿元。

### Page 3

Investment Thesis and Price Target Risks and Methodology

Investment thesis - Hiwin Corp 
Hiwin is the second-largest global linear motion supplier with 18% market share in 2024. 
It continues to witness decelerating market share growth from 2020 when it started to 
pursue a deleveraging strategy.   
Although we expect Hiwin to continue to benefit from rising demand from the 
semiconductor/EV industry given the upgrading trend, we are Sell rated, given: (1) we 
expect a weak end-market demand recovery and high inventory levels for the near term, 
leading to high pricing pressure and a stagnant utilization rate; (2) continued 
overcapacity issues in mainland China, where the company has higher exposure (45% in 
2024) vs. peers such as THK (24%), and we expect this could lead to further 
deterioration in Hiwin’s revenue/profitability outlook in the near term; and (3) the 
negative impact from its long-term deleveraging strategy. Hiwin continues to pay back 
long-term debt and expand capacity to better compete with peers, which we believe will 
further impact EPS growth and valuation. The stock is currently trading above its 
historical average valuation (33.0x). We expect its P/E multiple to derate once the 
market begins to adjust for a prolonged downcycle and expect downgrades to 
consensus EPS growth/ROE estimates, suggesting high downside risk to Hiwin’s 
valuation.  
Price target risks and methodology - Hiwin Corp 
Valuation methodology: Our 12m TP of NT$180 is based on a target P/E of 20x 2027E 
EPS (in-line with Hiwin’s average downcycle valuation, or 1std lower than the historical 
average P/E for the past 10 years).  
Key upside risks: (1) Stronger-than-expected EV & semi CAPEX growth; (2) a favorable

Ratings, coverage universe and related definitions
Buy (B), Neutral (N), Sell (S) Analysts recommend stocks as Buys or Sells for inclusion on various regional Investment Lists. Being assigned a Buy or Sell
on an Investment List is determined by a stock’s total return potential relative to its coverage universe. Any stock not assigned as a Buy or a Sell on an
Investment List with an active rating (i.e., a  stock that is not Rating Suspended, Not Rated, Early-Stage Biotech, Coverage Suspended or Not Covered), is

deemed Neutral. Each region manages Regional Conviction Lists, which are selected from Buy rated stocks on the respective region’s Investment Lists
and represent investment recommendations focused on the size of the total return potential and/or the likelihood of the realization of the return across
their respective areas of coverage.  The addition or removal of stocks from such Conviction Lists are managed by the Investment Review Committee or
other designated committee in each respective region and do not represent a change in the analysts’ investment rating for such stocks.

Total return potential represents the upside or downside differential between the current share price and the price target, including all paid or
anticipated dividends, expected during the time horizon associated with the price target. Price targets are required for all covered stocks. The total
return potential, price target and associated time horizon are stated in each report adding or reiterating an Investment List membership.
Coverage Universe: A list of all stocks in each coverage universe is available by primary analyst, stock and coverage universe at

https://www.gs.com/research/hedge.html.

### Page 3

Auras Technology (3324.TWO)
11 November 2026 Citi Research
3
Auras Technology
Valuation
We value Auras using a PE-based approach, reflecting its accelerating earnings trajectory and rising share in liquid cooling
market of AI servers. Our TP of NT$2,000 is based on 20x 2027E EPS, which we believe is justified given the company’s
expanding server revenue base, deeper engagement with CSP customers, and broader product portfolio across cold plates,
CDMs, and CDUs.

Risks
This stock is High Risk based upon our quantitative model, but assigning a High Risk rating is not supported in our view by
other qualitative factors such as rapid RVL progress and Thailand expansion, and so a High Risk rating has not been applied.
Key downside risks to our investment thesis and target price include: 1) slower adoption of liquid cooling; 2) heightened price
competition; 3) lower-than-expected share in liquid cooling components; and 4) emerging alternative cooling technologies.

Guide to Citi Research Fundamental Research Investment Ratings:
Citi Research stock recommendations include an investment rating and an optional risk rating to highlight high risk stocks.

Risk rating takes into account both price volatility and fundamental criteria. Stocks will either have no risk rating or a High risk
rating assigned.
Investment Ratings: Citi Research investment ratings are Buy, Neutral and Sell. Our ratings are a function of analyst
expectations of expected total return ("ETR") and risk. ETR is the sum of the forecast price appreciation (or depreciation) plus
the dividend yield for a stock within the next 12 months.  The target price is based on a 12 month time horizon. The Investment

rating definitions are: Buy (1) ETR of 15% or more or 25% or more for High risk stocks; and Sell (3) for negative ETR. Any covered
stock not assigned a Buy or a Sell is a Neutral (2). For stocks rated Neutral (2), if an analyst believes that there are insufficient
valuation drivers and/or investment catalysts to derive a positive or negative investment view, they may elect with the approval
of Citi Research management not to assign a target price and, thus, not derive an ETR. Citi Research may suspend its rating

and target price and assign “Rating Suspended” status for regulatory and/or internal policy reasons. Citi Research may also
suspend its rating and target price and assign "Under Review" status for other exceptional circumstances (e.g. lack of
information critical to the analyst's thesis, trading suspension) affecting the company and/or trading in the company's
securities. In both such situations, the rating and target price will show as “—“ and “-“ respectively in the rating history price

chart. Prior to 11 April 2022 Citi Research assigned “Under Review” status to both situations and prior to 11 Nov 2020 only in
exceptional circumstances. As soon as practically possible, the analyst will publish a note re-establishing a rating and
investment thesis. Investment ratings are determined by the ranges described above at the time of initiation of coverage, a
change in investment and/or risk rating, or a change in target price (subject to limited management discretion). At times, the

expected total returns may fall outside of these ranges because of market price movements and/or other short-term volatility
or trading patterns. Such interim deviations will be permitted but will become subject to review by Research Management.
Your decision to buy or sell a security should be based upon your personal investment objectives and should be made only
after evaluating the stock's expected performance and risk.
Catalyst Watch/Short Term Views (“STV”) Ratings Disclosure:

Catalyst Watch and STV Upside/Downside calls: Citi Research may also include a Catalyst Watch or STV Upside or Downside
call to indicate the analyst expects the share price to rise (fall) in absolute terms over a specified period of 30 or 90 days in
reaction to one or more specific near-term catalysts or events impacting the company or the market. A Catalyst Watch will be
published when Analyst confidence is high that an impact to share price will occur; it will be a STV when confidence level is

moderate. A Catalyst Watch or STV Upside/Downside call will automatically expire at the end of the specified 30/90 day
period. The Catalyst Watch will also be automatically removed if share price performance (calculated at market close) exceeds
15% against the direction of the call (unless over-ridden by the analyst). The analyst may also remove a Catalyst Watch or STV
call prior to the end of the specified period in a published research note. A Catalyst Watch/STV Upside or Downside call may be

### Page 1

Taiwan Information Technology

What’s new: MPI hosted its investor meeting on 16 November. 3Q26 
revenue was TWD5.9bn (+12.8% QoQ), 3.2% below our forecast but 2.1% 
above the Bloomberg consensus. Gross margin rose 3.3pp QoQ to 61.7%, 
beating our/consensus forecasts by 2.2pp, driven by a better product mix. 
Operating margin reached 38.6% (+4.1pp QoQ), 1.4/3.2pp above 
our/consensus forecasts. Management guided for 4Q26E/2027E revenue 
growth of 5-10% QoQ/50% YoY, with gross margin of c.62%/above 60%, 
respectively. We believe the company’s guidance is conservative and 
forecast 4Q26/2027 revenue to be TWD6.7bn/TWD37.7bn, with gross 
margin of 61.8%/61.5% and operating margin of 39.5%/39.9%, 
respectively.

What’s the impact: Probe card capacity expansion on track. MPI 
maintained its capacity expansion plan, targeting monthly VPC/MEMS 
capacities of 2.0m/3.5m probes by end-2026 and 2.5m/6.0m by end-2027. 
Management plans to release its final 2027 expansion plan by end-
December, with potential upside to current targets. Demand remains solid, 
with capacity still insufficient to meet customer needs and no signs of order 
cuts, per management. We believe testing intensity is the key driver of 
sector growth and we expect MPI's diversified customer base to limit the 
impact of order fluctuations from any single customer or project.

CPO contribution likely to accelerate in 2028. Although the market 
seems conservative about its CPO ramp-up, the company claimed that 
Insertion 2 qualification at a tier-1 customer remains on track, with 
qualification results expected by end-December and potential mass 
production orders in 1H27. For Insertion 3, qualification is progressing with 
multiple customers, with potential production orders in 1H27. Given 
qualification lead times, we forecast the CPO testing machine to contribute 
5-13% of revenue over 2027-28, aligned with management’s expectation 
that CPO will contribute a double-digit % in 2028.

What we recommend: We lower our 2026-28E EPS by 6-13% to reflect 
higher opex and cut our 4-quarter-forward target PER multiple from 65x to 
45x to reflect recent macro uncertainties, peer derating and moderating 
earnings growth. As a result, we lower our 12-month TP to TWD5,625 (from 
TWD8,415), while reaffirming our Buy (1) rating. Our target multiple implies 
a c.0.6 PEG based on our 2026-28E EPS CAGR of 73%. Given its 
diversified customer base and continued AI/HPC demand growth, we view 
current levels as offering an attractive risk-reward profile.

How we differ: Our 2027-28E EPS are 4-5% below the Bloomberg 
consensus, likely on our more conservative opex assumptions.

16 November 2026

Solid fundamentals; attractive entry post derating

➢ Capacity expansion on track; AI/HPC demand remains intact 
➢ CPO contribution likely to become more significant in 2028  
➢ Reaffirming our Buy (1) rating; lowering 12-month TP to TWD5,625

Company profile 
Founded in Hsinchu in 1995, MPI is a leading global probe card supplier offering CPC, VPC and MEMS 
solutions, alongside photonics, semiconductor and thermal testing equipment. Growth is driven by 
expanding AI/HPC demand, rising MEMS adoption, capacity expansion, deeper vertical integration and 
emerging opportunities in CPO testing.

ESG risks
Risks Management Analyst comments
G
Executive/board
quality 2
MPI maintains a robust governance framework with an independent board, specialized
committees, and regular ESG oversight to strengthen transparency, accountability, and long-
term sustainability.
Capital management 2 The company follows a prudent financial strategy, balancing growth investments, R&D spending, and cash flow management to enhance long-term shareholder value.
Related party &
transaction 2
MPI enforces strict conflict-of-interest and related-party transaction controls through board
oversight, audit committee review, disclosure requirements, and recusal mechanisms to ensure
transparency, fairness, and the protection of shareholder interests.
S Supply chain management 1 MPI promotes responsible supply chain management through supplier assessments, ESG integration, sustainability audits, and ongoing engagement to enhance supply chain resilience.
E Water & wastewater management 2 MPI implements water conservation and wastewater control measures, focusing on efficient resource utilization, pollution prevention, and sustainable water management practices.

張予柔 yurou.chang@sinopac.com 
本次報告：法說會

貿聯-KY (3665 TT) 
併購綜效超預期，獲利動能再加速

永豐觀點 
併購 Interplex、新富生貢獻雙雙超預期，獲利上修循環啟動。 
 投資評價與建議 
重申買進建議：研究部本次上修貿聯財務預估：(1) Interplex 資料通訊業務
(Datacom Business)營收規模大幅超標：10 月首個完整併表月營收約新台幣 21
億元，大幅優於研究部原先預期之約 13 億元，研究部同步上修其 2026~2028 年
營收與獲利貢獻預估。(2) 新富生月營收規模自年初以來已成長逾一倍，憑藉資料
中心高階光纖跳線的製造與品質優勢，持續拓展新客戶，研究部預估其 2027 年營
收可持續倍增。(3) 產品組合轉佳、營運槓桿持續發揮：3Q26 毛利率季增 1.8 個
百點至 32.53%，且營業費用率降至約 12%，帶動營益率創 20.78%新高。綜合預
估貿聯 2026-2028 年集團營收分別為 1,074、1,659、2,161 億元(+51%、+55%、
+30%YoY)；稅後淨利 150、267、356 元(+66%、+79%、+33%YoY)，完全稀
釋後 EPS 分別為 75.25、134.07 及 178.24 元。考量 AI 供應鏈遇短期逆風，影響
評價表現，研究部將本益比調整為 18 倍，維持買進建議，目標價 2,810 元(2027
年及 2028 年之平均預估 EPS 156.16*18XPER)。

ESG 評析 
貿聯企業永續評鑑整體為 B+等，於 SASB 產業永續議題與跨產業五大永續面向平
均得分分別為 B 等與 A 等。 
SinoPac+ ESG 評鑑系統評等

貿聯-KY 是全球領先的連接器公司之一：貿聯-KY 是全球領先的連接器公司之一：
貿聯-KY 總部設於加州 Fremont，3Q26 產品比重為資訊科技與數據傳輸 48％、
工業用 31％、車用 8％、電器 12％；若以事業別區分，HPC 占 46％、半導體設
備(SPE)占 13％、其他事業占 41％。貿聯主要核心能力，在於高技術及客製化開
發零組件製造，產品開發以多角化及差異化策略，2026/1 完成收購新富生光電
(XFS)，2026/9/23 完成收購 Interplex 集團資料通訊業務(Interplex Datacom)，
產品線由電源與數據連接延伸至光纖跳線、機殼及機櫃等精密機構件，朝 AI 機櫃
次系統解決方案供應商發展。 
3Q26 財報公布，優於市場預期，營收與獲利同創新高，營益率首度突破 20%：
3Q26 營收 278.75 億元(+19.7% QoQ、+51.4% YoY)，毛利率 32.53%，季增 1.8
個百分點；營業利益率 20.78%，季增 3.6 個百分點、年增 1.2 個百分點，為公司
首度突破 20%，獲利表現優於市場預期。成長主要來自 HPC，3Q26 HPC 營收季
增 30%、年增 61%，營收比重由 2Q26 的 42%回升至 46%，其中電源產品為成
長主力，帶動產品組合改善；半導體設備營收亦季增 11%、年增 70%，各事業全
面成長。費用方面，營收規模擴大但營業費用未等比例增加，營業費用率降至約
12%的歷史低點，顯示獲利率改善來自規模效益與費用效率，而非一次性因素，具
備延續性。稅後淨利 42.93 億元(+44.0% QoQ、+63.3% YoY)，稀釋後 EPS 21.51
元，營收、淨利與 EPS 皆創歷史新高；前三季累計 EPS 48.04 元，已超越 2025 全
年之 46.07 元。

營運現況與分析

### Page 3

3

研究部上修 2026~2028 年營收獲利預估，原因有三： 
1. Interplex 資料通訊業務(Datacom Business)營收提前兩年達標：Interplex
的資料通訊業務(Datacom Business)自 9/23 起併表，3Q26 僅貢獻 7 天營
收；10 月為首個完整併表月，帶動貿聯 10 月營收達 118.46 億元，月增 26.9%、
年增 80.0%，其中 Interplex 貢獻 6,700 萬美元(約新台幣 21 億元)。對照公司
宣布併購時揭露之年營收 3.92 億美元(月平均約 3,300 萬美元)，Interplex 月
營收已倍增，大幅優於市場預期。研究部看好併購綜效將逐步顯現：Interplex
主要供應 AI 伺服器機殼、機櫃等精密機構件，研究部認為其營收較併購時大
幅成長，主因 AI 機櫃出貨量較年初明顯放大，且併入貿聯後，Interplex 可運
用集團的資金、客戶關係與製造資源擴大接單，雙方產品整合後可提供涵蓋配
電、數據連接、機構件與液冷的機櫃次系統方案，單一 AI 機櫃之可供應產值
明顯擴大。研究部本次上修 2027、2028 年 Interplex Datacom Business 為
貿聯帶來的營收預估，分別為 268、308 億元(原預估 183、250 億元)。 
2. 新富生光通訊規模快速放大，營收占比持續提升：新富生(XFS) 專精於光纖互
連產品的設計與製造，主要產品包括單芯／雙芯跳線、光纖尾纖、多芯光纖以
及 MTP/MPO 高密度連接器，月營收規模自 2026 年初以來成長逾一倍，獲
利能力同步改善，展現規模化高精密光學互連業務的效益。資料中心對光纖組
件的品質與一致性要求遠高於一般應用，新富生在高階光纖跳線的製造經驗與
品質優勢，加上貿聯集團的客戶網絡與全球產能布局，有助其持續拓展新客戶。
隨 AI 資料中心機櫃間光纖連接需求成長，研究部預估新富生 2027 年營收可
望再翻倍增長，且其獲利率改善亦有助提升貿聯整體獲利結構。 
3. 3Q26 營益率創新高，營運槓桿持續發揮：3Q26 營業費用率約 12%，較 1H26
的 13.7%明顯下降，顯示貿聯在承接更大規模、更複雜的客戶專案時，製造、
工程與行政職能的效率同步提升，費用無須等比例增加。4Q26 起 Interplex
完整併表，營收規模再擴大，研究部預期營益率改善幅度可望大於毛利率，稅
後淨利成長幅度將高於營收。 
HPC 電源成長最強，新平台升級為下一波動能：3Q26 HPC 營收年增 61%、季增
30%，前三季累計年增 77%。隨運算功耗提升，電源產品切入規格更高的新一代
平台；數據連接方面，貿聯同時布局銅纜與光纖，因應不同傳輸距離與頻寬需求。
貿聯於 10 月 OCP Global Summit 2026 展出適用於 OCP 及 NVIDIA MGX 機櫃
之 800V/400VDC 高功率電源方案，以及 1.6T 液冷交換器 OSFP 連接器、機櫃管
理控制與漏液偵測系統，產品範圍由電源線材延伸至配電、液冷與監控。研究部認
為，隨 Vera Rubin 平台放量及 HVDC 架構導入，單一機櫃的電源產品規格與產值
將持續提升，為 2027 年 HPC 成長主軸。 
次世代光通訊布局：貿聯 2026 年先後於 OFC 展示 Optical Shuffle、於 Computex
展示 CPO 交換器托盤方案；9 月於 ECOC 2026 展示與 ams OSRAM 合作之
microVCSEL 光學互連方案，採用 850nm 二維可定址 microVCSEL 陣列，以多通
道、低速率(wide-and-slow)架構降低功耗與延遲，貿聯負責光纖陣列組裝、光學
封裝、光學測試與量產製造；10 月於 OCP Global Summit 2026 展出 CPO、
800G/1.6T 及 XPO 光收發模組、光纖陣列(FAU)與多芯光纖連接等方案。公司表
示產業未來可能並行採用多種光學架構，貿聯以製造與整合能力參與各技術路線，
不需押注單一方案。 
半導體設備成長幅度為產業兩倍以上：3Q26 半導體設備(SPE)營收年增 70%、季
增 11%，營收占比由 3Q25 的 11%提升至 13%，前三季累計年增 48%。根據國
際半導體產業協會（SEMI）於 2026 年 7 月公布之預測，全球前段晶圓製造設備

### Page 4

4

(WFE)市場 2026 年預估年增 23.1%至 1,439 億美元，貿聯 SPE 成長幅度約為產業
的兩倍；自 2023 年以來貿聯之 SPE 業務成長即大幅超越整體 WFE 市場，超額成
長主因貿聯在客戶端的角色由供應線材零組件，擴大至高階組裝、模組與次系統製
造、測試及系統整合，應用以薄膜沉積與蝕刻設備為主，微影與檢測設備為輔；隨
設備製程日益複雜，客戶傾向將更多製造工作委外，以集中資源於核心技術，貿聯
單一設備平台之內含價值隨之提升。SEMI 預估 WFE 市場 2027 年及 2028 年將分
別成長 21.8%及 14.1%，2028 年規模可望突破 2,000 億美元，主要由先進邏輯、
HBM 等先進記憶體投資帶動；公司亦表示，許多客戶對設備投資的強勁預期延伸
至 2030 年以後，且客戶正主動協助貿聯擴充產能，以支援其自身長期成長。貿聯
在 SPE 領域的投資以擴充製造產能為主，Interplex 的精密機械加工與次系統整合
能力亦可與 SPE 業務互補，為 HPC 以外的第二成長引擎。 
隨伺服器架構升級驅動產值倍增，2027~2028 年獲利成長路徑清晰，重申買進： 
研究部認為，貿聯本次財報的關鍵不只是單季獲利優於預期，而是兩項併購同時優
於預期，帶動營收基期結構性墊高：10 月營收 118.46 億元，較 3Q26 月平均約
93 億元高出約 27%。在 AI 機櫃同時需要電源、數據傳輸(銅纜與光纖)與精密機構
件的趨勢下，貿聯透過 Interplex 與新富生補齊產品版圖，單一機櫃可供應產值持
續擴大；而未來一年內，Interplex 完整併表(4Q26)、新富生營收比重提升(2027)、
Vera Rubin 平台與 HVDC 電 源 放 量 (2027~2028) 及 次 世 代 光 互 連 產 品
(2H27~2028)等成長催化劑將陸續到位，獲利上修動能明確。 
評價方面，短期因市場擔憂 GPU 產品推出節奏可能放緩，台灣 AI 供應鏈評價短
期受到壓抑。研究部認為，CSP 資本支出與現行平台訂單短期不受影響，且貿聯的
客戶與產品布局相對分散：公司採取平台中立策略，產品同時供應 GPU 與 ASIC
架構之 AI 機櫃，並服務多家雲端服務業者；銅纜與光纖、電源與機構件等產品線
亦不依賴單一技術路線；此外，半導體設備與其他事業合計仍占營收逾五成，營運
受單一平台影響的程度低於純 GPU 供應鏈。惟考量主要客戶經營層異動可能影響
新平台推出節奏，研究部於評價上給予折價，本益比由前次之 25 倍調整為 18 倍。
搭配本次上修後之 EPS，維持買進建議，目標價 2,810 元(2027 年及 2028 年之平
均預估 EPS 156.16*18XPER)。

營收走勢圖

研究員/丁宥霖 yulin.ting@uni-psg.com

貿聯 3Q26 財 報 優 於 預 期 ， 營 業 利 益 率 創 歷 史 新 高 ， 加 上 
Interplex 併表效益優於原先預估，為後續營運增添新亮點。展
望未來，研究部持續看好貿聯中長期成長潛力，主要考量：(1) 隨 
AI 機櫃功耗持續提升，電力傳輸規格朝高電流、高壓化發展，帶
動 Power Whip、Busbar 等產品升級，單櫃電源產值可望倍增；
(2) 高速互連朝 1.6T 以上演進，AEC 與高階光纖業務接續放
量，推升資料傳輸產值；(3) Interplex 併購補足精密機構能力，
結合既有電力及高速互連布局，貿聯可望由零組件供應商逐步延
伸至機櫃次系統整合，擴大單一客戶供應範圍及長期成長商機。
研究部將 Interplex 納入預估，上修 2026、2027 年稀釋後 EPS 
至 73.49 元（+67.4% YoY，原估 68.77 元）及 145.24 元
（+103.2% YoY，原估 117.82 元）。惟考量近期 AI 資料中心建
置不確定性升高，可能使新平台導入與機櫃出貨時程遞延，壓抑
評價表現，研究部將本益比由 25 倍下修至 20 倍，以 2027 年 
EPS 為評價基礎，目標價 2,950 元，投資評等由「買進」調升至
「強力買進」。

重點摘要： 
1. 3Q26 營業利益率創歷史新高，財報優於預期 
貿聯 3Q26 營收 278.75 億元（+19.7% QoQ、+51.4% YoY），符
合預期。其中 HPC 營收成長強勁（+30% QoQ、+61% YoY），營收
占比由 2Q26 的 42% 提升至 46%。隨 AEC 拉貨恢復正常，上半
年產線未達經濟規模的不利影響逐步消除，帶動整體毛利率回升
至 32.53%（+1.80ppt QoQ、−0.72ppt YoY）；加上營收規模擴
大，營業費用率降至 11.75%（−1.76ppt QoQ、−1.97ppt YoY），
推升營業利益率至 20.78%（+3.56ppt QoQ、+1.24ppt YoY），創
歷史新高。營業利益達 57.92 億元（+44.5% QoQ、+61.0% YoY），
優於原估的 52.50 億元。業外因匯兌損失及利息費用合計損失 
2.30 億元，稅後淨利達 42.90 億元（+43.9% QoQ、+63.2% YoY），
優於原估的 39.78 億元，稀釋後 EPS 為 20.34 元，優於原估
的 19.45 元。

### Page 2

請參見末頁重要批露及免責聲明  2

2. Interplex 併表貢獻超預期，上修 4Q26 營收與獲利 
貿聯 10 月營收 118.46 億元（+26.9% MoM、+80.0% YoY），創
歷史新高，主要受惠 Interplex 於 9 月底完成交割，10 月首
度完整併表即貢獻營收 6,700 萬美元，約為併購公告時近 12 個
月平均月營收的一倍。Interplex 為 NVIDIA NVLink Switch 機
殼主要供應商，受惠 AI 機櫃需求強勁，併表貢獻高於原先預估。
考量併購效益優於預期，加上既有業務持續成長，研究部上修 
4Q26 營收至 349.76 億元（+25.5% QoQ、+76.8% YoY，原估 
302.69 億元）。預估毛利率為 32.49%（−0.04ppt QoQ、+0.56ppt 
YoY），大致持平前季；隨營收規模擴大及 Interplex 較低費用
率貢獻，營業費用率降至 10.85%，推升營業利益率至 21.64%
（+0.86ppt QoQ、+3.27ppt YoY，原估 20.0%）。預估 4Q26 稅
後淨利達 55.28 億元（+28.9% QoQ、+101.8% YoY），稀釋後 EPS 
由原估 22.38 元上修至 26.21 元（ +28.9% QoQ、+101.8% YoY）。

3. AI 機櫃供電架構升級，電源單櫃產值與市占率同步提升 
隨 AI 伺服器運算密度持續提高，機櫃功耗由 GB200 世代約 
120kW，朝 Vera Rubin 世代 200kW 以上發展，電力傳輸規格同
步升級。在既有低壓直流架構下，功耗提升帶動電流需求增加，
Power Whip 與 Busbar 須升級至更高載流規格，推升單櫃電源
產值；隨機櫃功率持續攀升，傳統 54VDC 架構面臨銅材用量、配
電損耗及機櫃空間限制，NVIDIA 已推動 800VDC 高壓直流架構，
透過提高配電電壓、減少電力轉換環節，支援未來 MW 級 AI 機
櫃需求。貿聯除受惠電源產品規格升級，於 Vera Rubin 世代的 
Busbar 市占率亦可望明顯提升，推動單櫃電源產值較 GB 世代
倍增。考量新平台相關出貨預計於 1H27 起逐季放量，研究部預
估 2027 年電源營收成長約 114% YoY；中長期隨 800VDC 架構
逐步導入，既有 Power Whip、Busbar 及高電流連接技術可望延
伸至新一代配電系統，持續擴大供應商機。

### Page 3

請參見末頁重要批露及免責聲明  3

4. 高速互連升級至 1.6T，AEC 與高階光纖雙軌成長 
隨 AI 資料中心傳輸頻寬由 800G 朝 1.6T 以上升級，高速互連
對訊號完整性、傳輸距離及功耗的要求持續提高。短距離傳輸仍以
銅纜具成本優勢，較長距離則由光纖滿足頻寬與訊號傳輸需求，預
期 AEC 與光互連將持續並存。AEC 方面，1.6T 新規格因材料成本
及組裝難度提高，產品單價與毛利率可望優於前代，預計於 4Q26 
至 1Q27 逐步導入量產，研究部預估 2027 年 AEC 營收成長約 
41% YoY，主要受惠高階產品占比提升。光纖方面，新富生主要供
應 AI 資料中心機櫃間傳輸所需的 MPO、MT 高密度光纖跳線及連
接器組件，受惠高速光纖互連需求成長，月營收較 2026 年初成長
逾一倍，獲利同步改善。中長期來看，貿聯亦積極布局 Optical 
Shuffle、CPO 交換器方案及 Micro-VCSEL 光互連技術，逐步由傳
統線纜組裝延伸至高階光學組件，為後續成長開拓更多商機。

圖一、貿聯-KY 1Q25～4Q27F HPC 營收結構

資料來源：統一投顧預估

5. 半導體設備需求強勁，非 HPC 業務維持穩健 
貿聯 3Q26 半導體設備營收成長 11% QoQ、70% YoY，為非 HPC 業
務主要成長動能。隨先進製程及半導體設備投資持續推進，客戶需
求由傳統線束延伸至設備模組、精密組裝及次系統整合，帶動單機
供應產值提升。貿聯已深入薄膜沉積與蝕刻設備供應鏈，並逐步拓
展至微影及檢測設備，擴大產品應用範圍。其他業務方面，工廠自
動化、醫療、能源及車用等終端需求維持穩健，提供多元營收來源。

### Page 4

請參見末頁重要批露及免責聲明  4

6. 上修 2026～2028 年獲利預估，營運槓桿與併購效益持續擴
大 
研究部將 Interplex 併表貢獻納入預估，上修貿聯 2026 年營收
至 1,069.96 億元（+50.2% YoY，原估 1,021.18 億元），毛利率
估 31.4%，稀釋後 EPS 73.49 元（+67.4% YoY，原估 68.77 元）。
展望 2027 年，受惠 AI 電源及高速互連業務持續成長，加上 
Interplex 全年併表，預估營收達 1,710.84 億元（+59.9% YoY，
原估 1,517.46 億元）。隨高毛利 HPC 業務占比提升，毛利率可
望升至 34.3%（原估 34.0%）；加上營收規模擴大及 Interplex 
較低費用率貢獻，營業費用率降至 10.2%（原估 13.0%），推升營
業利益率至 24.1%（原估 21.0%），稀釋後 EPS 上修至 145.24 
元（+103.2% YoY，原估 117.82 元）。展望 2028 年，隨 AI 機
櫃供電架構朝 800VDC 演進、高速互連規格持續升級，加上 
Interplex 受惠機櫃需求成長及精密機構件供應範圍擴大，預期將
共同帶動營運成長。研究部預估營收達 2,136.69 億元（+24.9% 
YoY），稀釋後 EPS 196.20 元（+35.1% YoY，原估 171.03 元）。

圖二、貿聯-KY 2025～2028F 各應用別營收占比

資料來源：公司資料及統一投顧預估

7. 中長期成長趨勢明確，投資評等調升至「強力買進」 
研究部持續看好貿聯中長期成長潛力，隨 AI 機櫃電力與高速互
連規格持續升級，帶動單櫃供應產值提升，加上 Interplex 補足
精密機構能力，逐步擴大機櫃次系統供應範圍，有利深化客戶合作
關係並提升市占率。惟考量近期 AI 資料中心建置不確定性升高，
可能使新平台導入及機櫃出貨時程遞延，壓抑市場評價，研究部將
本益比由 25 倍下修至 20 倍。以 2027 年稀釋後 EPS 145.24 
元為評價基礎，目標價 2,950 元，投資評等由「買進」調升至「強
力買進」。

附錄一 ： 評 等 之 標 準 
評等 定義 
強力買進    預估未來 6 個月內的絕對報酬超過 25%以上 
買進 預估未來 6 個月內的絕對報酬 10%以上

주가 추이

3Q26 매출액과 영업이익은 비현금성 비용 항목 개선으로 시장 기대치
를 상회했으나, 차량 판매 감소와 인플레이션 등으로 연간 가이던스는 
하향되었습니다. 올해 생산 차질에 따라 이연된 신차 판매를 ’27년에 
확대하며 매출/이익 회복을 가시화하는 부분에 주목 필요합니다. 
매출 성장은 금융부문이, 영업이익은 자동차부문이 기여 
동사 3Q26 매출액은 45.1조원(-3.5% 이하 YoY), 영업이익 2.68조원
(+5.5%)을 기록하며 시장 기대치를 상회했다. 파업으로 인한 생산 차질
로 3Q 판매량 92.1만대(-11.3%)에 그치며 자동차부문 매출은 큰 폭으
로 감소(33조원, -10.0%)했으나, 금융부문 매출이 취급 자산 증가로 큰 
폭으로 성장(+27.8%)했다. 반면, 영업이익은 금리 상승에 따른 금융부문 
영업비용 확대 영향과 기타부문 실적 부진 발생과 달리 자동차부문은 전
년 동기 대비 관세율 차이에 따른 비용 감소와 판매보증 충당부채 전입
액 감소 등 우호적 환율효과로 큰 폭의 증가세(+24.8%) 기록했다. 
’26년 가이던스 하향, 구조적 반등 요인은 아직 
3Q 실적 발표에서 주목할 부분은 가이던스 하향과 현금흐름이다. 연간 
판매목표를 연초 415.8만대에서 400만대로 하향했고 영업이익률 목표
는 6.3~7.3%에서 5.5~6.5%로 0.8%p 하향했으며, ’26년 투자계획은 
17.8조원에서 16조원으로 낮췄다. 특히, 투자계획 하향은 차량 판매 매
출 감소 대비 인센티브, 원자재 가격 상승 등으로 현금흐름이 악화된 
부분에 기인한 것으로 판단된다. 실적발표에서 전사적 원가 최적화 활
동 추진을 강조했으나, 이는 좀 더 장기적 측면에서 효과가 가시화될 
것으로 판단되며, 인플레이션 및 원화절상 상황 고려 시 구조적 반등은 
단기적으로 쉽지 않을 수 있다. 
절치부심의 2027년, 목표주가 600.000원 유지 
그러나 매크로 상황을 제외하면  ’26년 하반기 생산 차질에 따른 신차 
판매효과가 ’27년으로 이연된 것이라는 점에 주목 필요하다.   ‘북미 HEV, 유럽 
BEV’ 전략 하에서 엘란트라, 투싼 풀세인지, GV80, G80 등 고수익 HEV 차종 
중심 미국 수요 대응을 통해 Mix 개선하는 모습이 확인되고, 생산이 본격화된 
아이오닉3 유럽 판매 확대 시 중국 로컬업체들의 공세를 방어하는 모습을 
확인하는 것이 가능할 것으로 판단한다. 노후차종 중심 운영에 따른 판매 둔화 
이외에 아직 근원적 판매 경쟁력 저하로는 판단하지 않는다.

[ Compliance Notice ] (공표일: 2026 년 10 월 30 일) 
이 자료는 조사분석 담당자가 객관적 사실에 근거해 작성하였으며, 타인의 부당한 압력이나 간섭없이 본인의 견을 정확하게 
반영했습니다. 본인은 이 자료에서 다룬 종목과 관련해 공표일 현재 관련 법규상 알려야 할 재산적 이해관계가 없습니다. 본인은 이 
자료를 기관투자자 또는 제 3 자에게 사전에 제공한 사실이 없습니다. (김성래) 
저희 회사는 공표일 현재 이 자료에서 다룬 종목의 발행주식을 1% 이상 보유하고 있지 않습니다. 
이 자료는 투자자의 증권투자를 돕기 위해 당사 고객에 한하여 배포되는 자료로서 저작권이 당사에 있으며 불법 복제 및 배포를 금
합니다. 이 자료에 수록된 내용은 당사 리서치센터가 신뢰할 만한 자료나 정보출처로부터 얻은 것이지만, 당사는 그 정확성이나 완
전성을 보장할 수 없습니다. 따라서 이 자료는 어떠한 경우에도 고객의 증권투자 결과와 관련된 법적 책임소재에 대한 증빙으로 사
용될 수 없습니다.

### Page 6

현대차 (005380) [한화리서치]

6 
[ 종목 투자등급 ] 
당사는 개별 종목에 대해 향후 1 년간 +15% 이상의 절대수익률이 기대되는 종목에 대해 Buy(매수) 의견을 제시합니다. 또한 절대수
익률 -15~+15%가 예상되는 종목에 대해 Hold(보유) 의견을, -15% 이하가 예상되는 종목에 대해 Sell(매도) 의견을 제시합니다. 밸류
에이션 방법 등 절대수익률 산정은 개별 종목을 커버하는 애널리스트의 추정에 따르며, 목표주가 산정이나 투자의견 변경 주기는 종
목별로 다릅니다.

[ 산업 투자의견 ] 
당사는 산업에 대해 향후 1 년간 해당 업종의 수익률이 과거 수익률에 비해 양호한 흐름을 보일 것으로 예상되는 경우에 Positive(긍정
적) 의견을 제시하고 있습니다. 또한 향후 1 년간 수익률이 과거 수익률과 유사한 흐름을 보일 것으로 예상되는 경우에 Neutral(중립적) 
의견을, 과거 수익률보다 부진한 흐름을 보일 것으로 예상되는 경우에 Negative(부정적) 의견을 제시하고 있습니다. 산업별 수익률 전
망은 해당 산업 내 분석대상 종목들에 대한 담당 애널리스트의 분석과 판단에 따릅니다.

[ 당사 조사분석자료의 투자등급 부여 비중 ] (기준일: 2026 년 9 월 30 일)

### Page 1

한화에어로스페이스 (012450)

EARNINGS REVIEW Company Analysis | 방위산업 | 2026. 11. 2  
방산 납품 계획에 따른 일시적 실적 급감

방산 납품 계획에 따른 일시적 실적 급감 
지상방산 매출 1조 2,450억원으로 전년비 41%, 영업이익 217억원으로 전년비 
96.2% 급감하며 영업이익률은 1.7% 기록. 실적 급감에 대해 사측은 올해 주요 사업
의 납품 일정이 4분기에 집중됨에 따라 3분기 인도 물량이 적었던 것과 대전 사업장 
생산 중단에 따른 영향 때문이라고 설명. 단, 4분기에는 당초 계획에 따라 주요 사업의 
납품 물량이 집중되어 있어 실적이 개선될 것이고 대전 사업장은 내년 상반기 내에 정
상 가돌 하는 것이 목표라고 밝힘 
수주잔고 증가세 지속으로 성장 지속 가능할 것 
3분기 말 수주잔고 35조원으로 전년비 12.9% 증가. 이를 통한 지상방산 부문 성장 
지속 가능할 것으로 판단. 참고로 사측은 내년 매출 15% 수준 성장 전망 유지. 하지
만 금리 상승에 따른 글로벌 업종 평균 밸류에이션 하락에 따른 방산 부문 적용 멀티
플 하향 조정 그리고 상장 연결회사(한화오션, 한화시스템, 쎄트렉아이)와 상장 비연결
회사(한국항공우주)의 주가 하락 반영 영향으로 목표주가는 하향 조정 
8월 미 육군 차륜형 자주포 사업에서 시제 사업자로 선정. 향후 K9MH는 미 육군의 
작전 운용 개념에 맞춰 맞춤 조정을 거친 후에 특별한 결함 혹은 결격 사유가 없으면 
최종 계약 체결 예정. 동시에 미 육군의 궤도형 자주포 사업에도 참여할 계획. 뿐만 아
니라 스페인 K9, 크로아티아 천무 등으로 신시장 개척 역시 지속되고 있다는 점도 긍
정적 
 Analyst 이재광 
rheejaekwang@lssec.co.kr

유틸리티업종

◼ 목표주가 85,000원(하향). 3분기 실적, 소규모 SMR 수주 등은 긍정적 
◼ 구체적인 미국 원전 사업개발 시작, 2H27 장납기품목 선발주 기대 
◼ 2027년 체코 원전 시공 입찰 재시도, 시공능력의 중요성 강조

목표주가 85,000원(하향). 3분기 실적 양호, 소규모 SMR 수주는 긍정적 
투자의견 ‘매수’ 유지, 목표주가 85,000원으로 하향. 이제는 성장 스토리보다
는 실질적인 원전 수주 확대가 필요한 상황. 의미 있는 수준의 원전 수주 확
대는 2H27부터 본격화될 가능성 존재. 다만, 단기적인 원전 수주 모멘텀 부
족 등을 감안해 6개월 목표주가를 현실화했음 
1) 3Q26 연결기준 영업이익 2,612억원(+91%, 이하 YoY), 에너빌리티 부문 
영업이익 1,211억원(+179%) 등 양호한 실적 기록. 이는 에너빌리티의 가스
터빈, 원전 기자재 매출 증가 등에 기인, 2) 10월 29일 NuScale과 1개 모듈 
주기기 공급 계약을 체결했으며, 4Q26 X-energy와 4개 모듈의 일부 장납기 
품목/소재 수주가 예상되는 등 2Q26 ~ 3Q26 TerraPower와의 일부 품목 계
약에 이어 개별 건의 규모는 작지만 다수의 SMR 관련 기자재 수주가 늘어나
는 점은 긍정적. 3) 최근 폴란드 AP1000 3기의 선발주 장납기 품목 확정(폴
란드 공급업체의 대상 생산 품목 확정, 원자로 압력용기 및 증기발생기는 미
확정)되었으며, 미국 원전 프로젝트가 구체화되기 시작. 4) 향후 AP1000향 
공급 기자재 품목 확대 가능성도 존재. 이는 2H27 본격적인 원전 수주금액 
확대로 이어질 것으로 기대

미국 원전은 구체적 사업개발 시작, 2H27 이후 장납기품목 선발주 기대 
2026년 6월 DOE의 ‘원전 공급망 금융지원 사업 발표(AP1000 10기)’, 8월 
TVA의 ‘2026 IRP’에 2040년까지 최대 원전 5.4GW 승인, 9월 한국과 미국 
상무부 간의 원전 투자 8기 MOU 체결 등을 통해 미국 원전 건설 재개 정책 
및 제도 개편이 구체화되고 강화되었음. 미국 원전 시장은 4Q26 ~ 2027년 
구체적인 사업개발 단계를 거쳐(부지 선정, 원전 운영 SPC 설립, 금융계약체
결, COL 신청 등), 2H27 ~ 2028년 장납기품목 선발주, 2028 ~ 2029년 건
설 계약 단계 진입 예상. 동사는 4Q26 미국 및 국내 가스터빈, 국내 공공 해
상풍력 프로젝트용 터빈, 폴란드 AP1000 주기기 등 미국 원전 이외 분야에
서의 수주 확대 예상. 현재 Rolls-Royce 등과도 SMR 주기기 공급 관련 논의 
중이며, NuScale SMR은 2027년 PPA 체결 시, COL 및 FID 이전 SMR 모듈 
11기(4Q26 1기 계약)의 수주가 가능할 것으로 기대

2027년 체코 원전 시공 입찰 재시도, 시공능력의 중요성 강조 
대우건설·두산에너빌리티 컨소시엄의 체코 원전 시공 계약 협상은 종료되었
지만, 2027년 7월 신규 시공사와 계약 예정. 동사는 입찰에 다시 참여할 예
정. 이번 이슈의 핵심 쟁점은 이미 체코 측과 공사비총액(Lump-sum) 계약을 
맺은 한수원이 시공사와 총액계약을 맺기를 원한 것임. 이번 이슈로 체코 두
코바니 원전 EPC 프로젝트 수익성에 대한 우려가 발생할 수 있지만, 실제 건
설 과정에서 추가 비용이 공사비 계약금액(예비비 포함)을 초과할지는 예단하
기 어려움. 하도급 관리, 공기 준수 등 시공능력의 중요성이 강조되는 상황. 
동사는 3년 전부터 체코에 시공 인력 상주하며 시공 환경 자료 확보, 초기 인
허가, 설계 업무 관여해 왔으며, 주기기 공급사로서 기자재 특성을 활용해 설
치까지 수행 가능한 경쟁력 등을 바탕으로 성공적 수주를 목표하고 있음

표 2. 웨스팅하우스의 미국 및 글로벌 신규 원전 프로젝트 현황 (초기 사업발굴 단계 제외)  
 프로젝트·사업 기수 발전용량 Cameco 분류 단계 및 비고 
미국 DOE 공급망 금융지원 사업 최대 10기 최대 11.5GW 장납기 품목 조달 금융지원, 7개 유틸리티와 논의 중 
 미 상무부 전략적 협력사업 최대 10기 최대 11.5GW 정부 협력사업(한국은 6기 투자/건설) 
 V.C. Summer 2·3호기 공사 재개 2기 2.3GW 장납기 품목 발주·EPC(LLI/EPC) 
폴란드 Lubiatowo-Kopalino 3기 3.5GW 장납기 품목 발주·EPC(LLI/EPC) 
불가리아 Kozloduy 7·8호기 2기 2.3GW 초기 서비스 계약(ESC) 
우크라이나 Khmelnytskyi 5·6호기 2기 2.3GW 초기 서비스 계약(ESC) 
정부 지원·협력 및 계약 기반 사업 소계  최대 29기 최대 33.4GW FEED·초기 사업발굴 제외 
FEED 사업 네덜란드, 슬로베니아, 핀란드, 스웨덴, 미국 합계 11기 합계 12.7GW 기본설계(FEED) 
총계 FEED 포함 사업군 최대 40기 최대 46.1GW 초기 사업발굴 제외 
자료: Cameco, 대신증권 Research Center

표 3. DOE의 미국 대형원전 공급망 금융지원 사업 
항목 공식 발표 내용 
조건부 대출 약정 규모 최대 175억 달러·US$17.5 billion 
지원 대상 최대 5개 프로젝트, 프로젝트당 AP1000 2기, 합계 10기 
프로젝트 소유 구조 Westinghouse와 해당 전력회사 또는 에너지기업이 공동 소유 
대출 접근 전 자기자본 요건 프로젝트당 Westinghouse 5억 달러, 상대 전력회사·에너지기업 5억 달러, 합계 10억 달러를 사전에 확약 
조달 방식 Westinghouse가 참여 기업과 함께 장납기품목을 고정가격으로 조달 
조달 시점 자기자본 확약 시점과 기타 조건에 따라 프로젝트별 구매를 순차적으로 진행

표 4. DOE의 미국 대형원전 공급망 금융지원 사업의 대상 장납기품목(LLI; Long-Lead Items) 
 품목 설명  대출과 관련된 확인 근거 
원자로압력용기 
(RPV; Reactor Pressure Vessel) 
핵연료와 노심을 수용하고 원자로 냉각재의 압력을 유지하는 
용기. 앞서 설명한 격납용기와 구분되는 원자로 주기기 DOE 공식 설명자료 — 2026.06.23 
증기발생기 
(SG; Steam Generator) 
원자로에서 나온 열을 전달받아 터빈을 돌릴 증기를 만드는 대
형 열교환기 DOE 공식 설명자료 — 2026.06.23 
사전제작 구조 모듈 
(PSM; Prefabricated Structural Modules) 
원전 건물의 벽체·바닥 등 구조물을 모듈로 미리 제작하는 방
식의 구성품. AP1000에는 강판과 콘크리트로 구성하는 구조 
모듈이 사용됨 
DOE 공식 설명자료 — 2026.06.23. 다만, 미국 대
출자료에 개별 모듈 코드까지 열거되지는 않음 
원자로냉각재펌프 
(RCP; Reactor Coolant Pump) 
원자로 냉각재를 순환시켜 원자로에서 발생한 열을 증기발생
기로 전달하는 펌프 DOE 공식 설명자료 — 2026.06.23 
격납용기 
(CV; Containment Vessel) 
원자로압력용기와 주요 설비를 둘러싸며 방사성 물질의 외부 
방출을 억제하는 대형 강철 밀폐 구조물 
DOE EDF 담당자의 직접 발언에서 확인 — 
2026.10.20 공개 인터뷰 
자료: DOE, 대신증권 Research Center

산업 투자의견 
- Overweight(비중확대) 
  : 향후 6개월간 업종지수상승률이 시장수익률 대비 초과 상승 예상

  : 향후 6개월간 업종지수상승률이 시장수익률과 유사한 수준 예상

  : 향후 6개월간 시장수익률 대비 10%p 이상 주가 상승 예상

  : 향후 6개월간 시장수익률 대비 -10%p~10%p 주가 변동 예상

Document 320: Cliff Notes_ the source and persistence of inflation (2026-11-18)

#cliffnotes #rba #australia #inflation #us #fomc #ecb #aud #usd
Cliff Notes: the source and persistence of inflation
By Ryan Wells, Illiana Jain & Elliot Clarke
11:16 October 30 2026 Share
Key insights from the week that was.
In Australia this week, all eyes were on the September CPI. Headline inflation rose 0.8% (4.3%yr) in
the month, largely owing to a 9.4% jump in fuel prices and a seasonal surge in domestic and
international holiday travel & accommodation. The underlying inflation pulse also proved hotter than

anticipated for Q3 overall, up 1.0% (3.7%yr).

### Page 2

While some parts of the basket are seeing disinflation – housing in particular looks to be cooling faster
than expected as builders lure sales with discounts – other components are still showing persistence.
Highlighting this is market services ex volatiles, which the RBA watches closely as an indicator of price
pressures related to domestic capacity. In Q3, this metric rose 1.2% (3.2%yr), and the monthly detail
points to momentum holding up – in line with our expectations for another chunky read in Q4.

With inflation running well above the RBA’s August forecasts, and some of the energy risks flagged
then materialising, we continue to expect the Monetary Policy Board will deliver a 25bp rate hike to
4.85% in November. Beyond November, another rate hike in February is a risk, but is not currently part
of our base case.
Across the Tasman, the RBNZ this week surprised market expectations for a 25bp rate hike, deciding
instead to wait for further information before taking the next step with policy. The decision was made

4:3, with the Governor in the ‘on hold’ camp. A 25bp hike in December to 3.00% and further tightening
in 2027 (Westpac NZ forecasts a peak of 4.00% in September) seems probable; however, each
decision will depend on fuel prices, inflation expectations and evidence of second-round pricing
pressures, along with the evolution of global economic risks.
In the US, the FOMC also held rates steady at its October meeting. Based on prior comments by key
members, the decision was not a surprise, and neither were Governor Cook and Cleveland Fed

President Hammack’s dissents in favour of a 25bp hike. The Committee remains positive on economic
activity, characterising it in the statement as “expanding at a strong pace” (an upgrade from “solid” in
September). Chair Warsh also subsequently emphasised the breadth of economic growth outside of
tech-related investment (evinced by Q3 GDP the day after; see below).
The Committee refrained from spotlighting key price risks in the statement. However, inflation “remains

elevated” and the FOMC are determined to achieve a “timely” and sustainable return of annual inflation
to 2.0%yr after 6 years above target. This guidance and the “accumulation of more information will help
the Committee determine the policy stance” suggests another 25bp hike is likely in December.
Thereafter, the FOMC will remain attentive to any broadening in inflation and related risks through
2027; although, in the absence of another shock, we expect them to remain on hold.

The Q3 GDP report certainly supported Chair Warsh’s view on the breadth of growth, the 3.4%
annualised gain for GDP benefitting from a 3.8% annualised increase in household consumption,
12.7% growth in non-residential investment as well as a robust increase in government spending.
Notably though, the contribution from business investment was effectively offset by net exports’ drag
given most of the componentry related to AI infrastructure investment is imported. This is not to say that

US firms won’t benefit from the investment once complete, but simply that much of the initial return
goes to the foreign businesses who provide the necessary capital equipment.
On consumption, the monthly PCE data implies downside risks remain for the consumer, the increase
in nominal spending seen in the month requiring dissaving and, on a real basis, consumption flat in the
month following a 0.5% gain in August. Cost-of-living pressures and uncertainty over the outlook is

### Page 3

Related articles
expected to see household consumption growth average around trend over the coming year, albeit with
considerable volatility quarter to quarter. This should see PCE inflation slow back towards its 2.0%yr
medium-term target as the FOMC desires, from 3.6%yr and 3.1%yr for the headline and core
measures respectively as of September.
Across the Atlantic, the ECB also left its key policy rates unchanged in October. Of note from the
meeting communications, energy prices continue to pose upside risks for inflation, but also downside

risks for growth. "While the full effects of the energy shock are still unfolding, underlying inflation has
remained broadly stable so far and wages do not show a material response". "Financing conditions
have [also] tightened", "which is likely to weigh on inflation and growth". In the press conference,
President Lagarde noted that, between the October and December meetings, a significant amount of
new economic information will be released, allowing forecasts and risks assessments to be tested.

Based on the October meeting communications, we continue to expect a rate hike at the December
meeting but believe this will be prove to be the last hike of the cycle. Note though, the spectrum of risks
Europe faces is broad and skewed towards inflation in the near term, hence the Council’s guidance will
remain hawkish. President Lagarde also announced today that she will step down as ECB President in
February ahead of the scheduled conclusion of her term next October.

2027 ANNUAL OUTLOOK

### Page 4

4
2027 ANNUAL OUTLOOK
As we approach 2027, we are faced with two competing trends. Capital, as defined by
interest rates, is delivering higher base returns, so our expectations for required returns to
invest have risen. At the same time the need for this capital continues to grow to deliver the
opportunities, and support the requirements, on the horizon. Companies and governments
are looking to investors to fund the opportunities and priorities ahead, at a time when capital

is more expensive and investment decisions must clear a higher bar.
In last year’s Outlook, we explored how three forces, artificial intelligence (AI), global
fragmentation and inflation, were defining a new market frontier. In 2027, that frontier must
be funded.
Companies need capital to realize the full potential of AI. Governments need capital to
strengthen security and resilience in a fragmented world. At the same time, inflation,
economic growth and central bank policy continue to shape the investment environment.

With cash earning a credible return, investors can afford to be discerning about where they
put capital to work.
That is especially true of the AI build-out. Markets are beginning to question whether the
scale of investment will ultimately pay off. We believe AI is a transformative technology
that will fundamentally change how we live and work. We also believe investors need to be
selective, with a focus on the companies that will define this new frontier.

In this environment, portfolio diversification remains critical. Forward-looking returns for
stock-bond portfolios are the most attractive in years, but market volatility creates a wide
range of possible outcomes. A diversified portfolio and a long-term perspective can help
investors navigate both.
The new market frontier is starting to take shape. As investors consider the opportunities
ahead, we are proud to serve as your financial partner and remain committed to helping you

achieve your long-term goals.
Foreword
David Frame
CEO,  J.P. Morgan Global Private Bank

Scorecard for the 2026 Outlook

### Page 6

6
2027 ANNUAL OUTLOOK
In last year’s Outlook, we highlighted their Promise and Pressure,
and since then the salience of these themes has only grown.
AI offered the potential for higher corporate profits and faster
productivity growth, but also the risk of overinvestment,
exuberance and disruption. Fragmentation favored resilience and
security, but at the expense of cost, efficiency and government
budgets. Inflation had retreated from its post-pandemic peak but

seemed structurally higher and more vulnerable to rolling shocks.
Through 2026, markets moved to reflect the world we envisioned,
in some cases more emphatically than we expected.
Demand for AI continued to far outstrip the supply of AI
computing power (“compute” in the tech sector’s shorthand).
This has rewarded the companies designing, producing and
providing the semiconductors, components and power required
to deploy AI at scale. AI model providers generated real revenue

as businesses began paying up for AI-driven productivity gains.
Companies viewed as vulnerable to disruption underperformed.
Conflicts in Iran and Ukraine continued to highlight deep
vulnerabilities in energy supply chains and defense inventories.
The companies that we believe strengthen economic and national
security resilience outperformed even as traditional defense
companies lagged. Inflation remained above central bank targets,
bond yields climbed to multi-decade highs and a global rate-hiking

cycle kicked into gear.
Against that backdrop, a traditional balanced portfolio seems
set to deliver high single-digit returns. Real assets (such as
infrastructure and commodities) and active strategies (such
as diversified hedge funds) have earned their keep as portfolio
diversifiers.
The three forces (AI, fragmentation, inflation) continue to define
the economic and market environment. And they’re interacting
in sometimes unexpected ways that investors need to understand.

One theme links the three: An enormous demand for capital in both
the private and public sectors is raising the cost of capital. (It’s the
law of supply and demand we remember from Economics 101.)
Investors like you set the price for that capital. That is a position
of strength. It also compels decisions that cannot be deferred.
As many investors look to protect—as well as expand upon—several
years of handsome equity market gains, those decisions become

even more important.
Technology companies need your capital—trillions of dollars’
worth—to fund the next phase of the AI build-out. Governments
need it to finance security, resilience and rearmament initiatives.
Consumers, companies and sovereigns all need it to adapt to an
inflationary, fragmented world.
But inflation, resilient economic growth and central bank policy
mean that cash is more attractive. Today, it earns a real return.
Every financing decision, from a hyperscaler’s data center to a

government’s defense budget to your own portfolio allocation,
now carries an opportunity cost that was obscured for most of
the last cycle.
The AI boom will continue only if investors like you are willing
to provide the financing to bridge the gap between infrastructure
spending and consistent revenue. You will share the cost of
security with governments through taxes, inflation or both, but
you can also decide to own the beneficiaries of that investment.

And a higher cost of capital means every one of these decisions
must now clear a higher bar.
The old investing playbook may not power your portfolio through
the current cycle, even if forward looking returns for traditional
stock-bond mixes are still attractive. In the post–global financial
crisis cycle, policymakers kept the cost of capital low to incentivize
risk taking and boost asset prices. Today, capital has a price. We
believe there is a wider range of possible outcomes and, in the

context of public markets that have rarely been this concentrated,
an increased need to consider exposures you may not have.
Capital is the bridge to a world that is more secure and
productive. Your capital sets the price of progress. Place it wisely.

### Page 11

INFLATION, CENTRAL BANKS AND THE COST OF CAPITAL11
In 2027, central banks will matter again. Before you place your capital, you must make a
judgment about how far central bank policy rates will rise. Inflation is key to that decision
for central banks—and for your portfolio.
We believe that the Federal Reserve and other global central banks are raising rates to
adjust their policy stance and influence inflation expectations. They are not looking to

lower demand. If we are right, the expansion can continue, and our constructive view
on multi-asset portfolios will hold. If we are wrong, the cycle could end with a hiking
campaign that breaks economic demand and pressures risk assets.
While U.S. inflation has remained above the Fed’s 2% target, that reflects supply-driven
factors such as energy prices. The labor market is stable and wage growth is decelerating.
Thus, we believe that inflation will begin to moderate. We see no sign of a nascent

wage-price spiral or overheating economy that the Fed would need to correct with an
aggressive rate hiking campaign.
The European Central Bank (ECB) seems to be running a similar playbook, signaling it will
continue hiking only if it sees further effects of higher energy prices. On the other hand,
the People’s Bank of China (PBoC), facing persistent disinflation, seems likely to keep
policy accommodative for the foreseeable future.
Last year, we argued that we are in a new

regime where transformative technologies
and a changing geopolitical environment
drive investment returns more than
traditional forces such as GDP growth
or central bank policies.

### Page 12

### Page 13

13
INFLATION, CENTRAL BANKS AND THE COST OF CAPITAL
13
Inflation containment: Cost of
capital, not the policy rate, is doing
the heavy lifting
In the post–global financial crisis period, unprecedented monetary
policy (zero and negative interest rates and quantitative easing)
coupled with robust forward guidance from the Fed anchored
sovereign rates and compressed credit spreads and equity risk
premiums. In other words, capital had an “artificial” price. Today,

after substantial interest rate hikes, quantitative tightening and
the discontinuation of forward guidance, the price of capital is
more real.
Investors have as much to do with that change as central bankers.
Even before September, when the Fed raised its policy rate 25
basis points (bps), yields were already on the rise. Year to date the
10-year U.S. Treasury has risen roughly 75bps to around 5%. Fifty
of those basis points arrived in September alone, placing the move

in the top decile of monthly increases since 1962. Credit spreads
have widened modestly, investment grade (IG) by around 10bps,
high yield by roughly 40bps.
Importantly, markets are expecting more than two additional
hikes in the current cycle (the next one in December). That means
a higher cost of capital already constrains growth and inflation,
even absent an aggressive Fed.
What are the risks to our view?
What might compel the Fed to kick off an aggressive hiking cycle?

A stronger-than-expected economy, with wages accelerating
ahead of productivity, could force the Fed’s hand. So too could a
sustained move in 5-year/5-year inflation breakevens above 3%.
Indeed, the trajectory of data center construction is important
for bond yields. Ten-year U.S. Treasury yields peaked as investors
began to question further AI capex in 2027. A reacceleration in AI
infrastructure investment could push yields higher.
If higher diesel prices depress economic demand, it would present

a particular challenge to policymakers. In the United States, the
cost of diesel has risen to nearly $6.30/gallon from ~$3.80 before
the Middle East conflict began. Diesel at $10/gallon for a quarter
could make a meaningful dent in economic activity. The Fed would
then have to choose between hiking into a weakening economy
or risking that inflation expectations move higher. This is just one
example of the potential that the next growth downturn could be

accompanied by stubborn inflation, which would pose a challenge
for more traditional portfolios.
INFLATION, CENTRAL BANKS AND THE COST OF CAPITAL

### Page 14

14
INFLATION, CENTRAL BANKS AND THE COST OF CAPITAL
Yes, inflation is still a threat
to your capital
Inflation is the foundation for our economic outlook, and we
believe it should also be reflected in the foundation of your
investment plan. Even though we expect inflation to moderate
through 2027, it still poses a key risk to the purchasing power
of your capital.
Many of our clients may also need to use a different benchmark
than policymakers and economists. From 2019 through 2025, the

headline PCE index rose at a ~3.5% annual rate. Over the same
period, based on our own clients’ spending data, average ticket
sizes on luxury goods purchases increased at more than a 6%
rate. Maintaining purchasing power for the goods and services
that matter most to you likely requires a higher hurdle than the
headline inflation index would suggest.

### Page 15

INFLATION, CENTRAL BANKS AND THE COST OF CAPITAL
The investment
implications
We believe that fixed income markets are
compensating investors appropriately to take on
the risks of sticky inflation and higher policy rates.
Investment grade yields in the United States are
above 6% for the first time since the global financial
crisis. Long-dated municipals yield roughly 5%,
equivalent to an ~8% taxable-equivalent yield.
At the end of the day, bond investing is math, and today’s bond

math is unusually friendly to investors.
At today’s all-in yields, rates would have to rise roughly another 100bps from here
to wipe out a year of IG carry, a worse outcome than even our bear case. Yields may
not have peaked for the cycle. But we believe that investors who add to high grade
credit today can realize solid returns over the medium term even if global central
banks tighten more than we expect and you experience short-term mark to market

losses along the way.

### Page 16

16
Beyond investment grade, preferred equity looks to be well 
positioned to deliver attractive returns to portfolios. In fact, we 
would rather fund U.S. and Canadian banks through preferred 
equity than common equity. Headline yields are around 7% and 
they are a tax efficient asset for U.S.-based investors. Many of the 
catalysts that could drive bank stock outperformance have played 
out. Still, collecting high-single-digit, tax-efficient returns  
by owning bank preferred stock seems attractive from a risk-
reward perspective. 
Private credit may also be a counterintuitive beneficiary of a rising 
rate environment. Consider: In direct lending, realized loan default 
rates range from 1% to 3%, but a broader “shadow” default rate
1

puts loans under stress at ~15% of the business development 
company (BDC) universe.
2
 Investors are compensated for taking 
on this risk: Spreads including discount at issue have widened 
by about 100bps year-to-date. At a ~5% default rate and ~50% 
recovery, loss-adjusted yields on senior-secured direct lending still 
clear ~7.5%.
3

Higher borrowing costs also present opportunity for distressed 
managers who can charge a high price for liquidity, and we are 
seeing new potential in places such as  private credit secondaries 
and asset-backed finance. 
In a world where capital once again has a price, fixed income is 
where that cost is being paid most explicitly to you.

### Page 17

### Page 18

18
We are more positive on core fixed income than we were last year.
Our long-term outlook for traditional multi-asset portfolios that
mix public equities with core fixed income suggests competitive
returns over the next 10-15 years.
However, we see three reasons to add other diversifiers
to portfolios.
First, in large part due to the uncertainty associated with
the potential payback of AI investment, there is a wide range
of possible outcomes.
Options that protect against a sharp decline in equities are trading

at their highest price of the year. Insurance protection against
default for investment grade companies that are borrowing to
finance the AI infrastructure build is approaching levels more
commonly seen in junk issuers. Assets like diversified hedge fund
strategies tend to perform well when volatility is elevated and
could thus help limit portfolio drawdowns. For example, macro
hedge funds have delivered positive performance on average
during quarters when both stocks and bonds are down.

Second, the threat of inflation shocks driven in part by
geopolitical fragmentation should lead to a positive
correlation between stocks and bonds.
That suggests that a traditional 60% stock/40% bond portfolio
is not prepared for a scenario where stocks and bonds are both
falling. Core real assets and commodities respond to inflation
differently than stocks and bonds. Both deserve the consideration
of your long-term capital.
Third, public equity markets have rarely been this

concentrated, largely because of the dominance of AI and
technology more broadly.
The 10 largest stocks in the S&P 500 account for nearly 40%
of the index, the highest share since at least 1980. Of course,
concentration in technology and AI-linked names has driven
strong performance, which we believe will continue.
Still, investors should consider alternative drivers of capital
appreciation. For instance, U.S. core private equity has a 24%
weight to technology and adjacent companies relative to the

S&P 500 at nearly 50%. In exchange for liquidity, private equity
provides the opportunity for capital appreciation without the
concentration of technology. Manager selection is crucial,
though, especially in an environment where software is at risk of
disruption and leverage-driven returns will be harder to generate.
A balanced portfolio
is only the beginning.

growing demand for capital from

### Page 21

21
Sovereigns need capital to defend themselves in a new era of
conflict, where $70,000 drones can be competitive with million-
dollar missile systems, defense inventories are depleted, and the
U.S. security umbrella is not what it once was.
4
Governments will
increasingly need capital to finance their AI ecosystems (“compute
sovereignty” may become a new catchphrase). And sovereigns
certainly need capital for energy security. Conflict in Iran and the

closure of the Strait of Hormuz were first a shock and then
a sobering reality, and the ongoing conflict has only exacerbated
the fragility of global energy supply chains, to say nothing of the
long-term threats from climate-related risks.
Where will the missing capital come from? You will be
asked to share the cost through some mix of tariffs, taxes,
inflation and higher rates.
Global fragmentation should also force an assessment of
geographic diversification. Be thoughtful about which currencies

you save in, which sovereigns you lend to, and consider the global
spectrum of potential beneficiaries.
Which sovereign blocs have
earned your capital?
Global fragmentation should force you to think more critically
about capital allocation across sovereign blocs. Traditional metrics
such as trade balances and policy credibility are useful, but you
must also consider a country’s natural resources and its exposure
to critical bottlenecks. Can a country defend itself? That question

has new resonance.
We suggest assessing sovereign risks against their vulnerability
to four criteria.
• Conflict. Can the sovereign provide its own security, or is
it dependent on outside support at prices that may prove
unsustainable?
• Compute. Does the sovereign have its own AI infrastructure
and research ecosystem? Is it prepared to defend against
AI-enabled cyberattacks?
• Chokepoints. Can the sovereign rely on domestically run
supply chains, or does it depend on foreign suppliers?

• Climate. Is the sovereign vulnerable to the negative impacts of
climate change, and is it positioned to invest in adaptation?
We think this framework can help identify which countries
and regions might be best positioned for the era of global
fragmentation. The bottom line is that all the blocs that we
care about as capital allocators have vulnerabilities to mitigate.
Concentration in one is probably not prudent.
4    Center for Strategic and International Studies. Drone Saturation: Russia’s Shahed

Campaign.  2025; CSIS. The Cost and Value in Air and Missile Defense Intercepts. 2025.

### Page 22

22
22
First, the United States seems relatively well-positioned to
weather geopolitical fragmentation over the long term, but
there are still critical risks to address. Fiscal capacity, resource
endowment and more limited bottleneck vulnerability all argue
in favor of Treasuries and the dollar. But there is a risk in central
banks and reserve managers diversifying their savings away
from the United States. U.S. policymakers and business leaders
are beginning to address competitive vulnerabilities in areas

such as semiconductor manufacturing, skilled engineering labor
and rare earths. The ongoing El Niño could cause disruption in
insurance markets and food prices, and we are waiting to see
the potential response from insurers and policymakers.
Europe: Market outlooks diverge among equity,
sovereign credit and corporate credit.
While bondholders are funding capital requirements into needed
resiliency spending, stockholders can benefit from that spending.

Europe’s challenges include elevated oil and gas prices
(most European nations are not energy self-sufficient), rising
government bond yields, high levels of debt and more frequent
and severe heatwaves and drought. In France, 10-year government
bonds now yield more than their Italian counterparts for the first
time since the creation of the eurozone, a roughly 130bps swing
from early 2024. (At the end of Q2, France’s public debt-to-GDP
ratio was 119% versus 140% for Italy.) French elections in 2027,

which will likely  produce a contest between candidates on the far
left and far right, loom large.
Across defense, AI, supply chains and climate, all global blocs have vulnerabilities to address

Here, we make a few observations
that can guide capital allocation decisions.

### Page 23

23
GLOBAL FRAGMENTATION
European policymakers on the national and pan-European level 
have called for greater investment in defense, technology and 
energy security in lieu of expanded welfare programs. We are 
also monitoring the potential for broader adaptation spending to 
mitigate the adverse impacts of a hotter, less predictable climate. 
German defense spending has doubled since 2022, with Germany 
notably harnessing its industrial infrastructure to diversify away 
from automobile manufacturing.
5
 But the broader push for 
investment has encountered political resistance and limitations  
in scaling up capacity. Only about a third of European Union 
counter-drone and short-range air defense programs are expected 
to be complete by 2030.
6

Given the emerging fiscal strains for European sovereigns, 
we prefer to place our capital in European multinational 
corporations over sovereign debt.
We continue to invest in European equity markets, where 
corporate earnings have proven stronger than expected. Capital 
has flowed to Europe’s beneficiaries of global fragmentation. 
The ranks include the dominant Dutch company that prints 
every advanced chip and French and British defense companies 
capturing rearmament orders.   
Asia: Different blocs, different trades
Geopolitical fragmentation plays differently across Asian markets. 
China is in many ways a beneficiary of the trend. It is an industrial 
and manufacturing power, with an enviable supply of energy. The 
country remains a major exporter (its trade surplus continues 
to set records) and it is building a parallel AI ecosystem with 
sophisticated low-cost models. But economic growth remains 
weak and uneven as its property sector continues to struggle and 
domestic consumption recovers slowly. In September, fixed asset 
investment fell 7.2%.
7

A U.S. or European investor who is considering allocating capital 
to China must also weigh the potential for alignment risk—that is, 
the potential for diverging geopolitical interests with China.  
On balance, we opt for a selective and relatively small allocation 
to Chinese markets, but you shouldn’t ignore it entirely. 
Korea, Taiwan and Japan sell what a fragmented world needs, 
e.g., semiconductors, AI infrastructure and shipbuilding 
equipment. Korea holds roughly 70% of global DRAM capacity, 
while Taiwan dominates advanced chip manufacturing.
8
We remain constructive on Korea and Taiwan equities and 
selectively positive on Japan.  
Latin America: Opportunities for capital in 
nearshoring and commodities
Latin America offers exposure to two related opportunities, the 
nearshoring that is accelerating amid global fragmentation and 
a bull market in commodities.
We see Mexico as a clear beneficiary of nearshoring. The United 
States–Mexico–Canada Agreement (USMCA) remains intact, 
despite political rhetoric from the U.S. administration. Mexico’s 
manufacturing base and deep capital market links to the United 
States underscore the country’s investment potential. That 
potential may increase as firms sell higher value-add exports and 
services into U.S. markets. In fact, tech products now make up 
nearly 50% of Mexico’s exports, and the United States receives 
40% of its imported servers from Mexico.
9
 For investors, it’s 
helpful, too, that the peso is a high-carry convertible currency. 
The rest of the region offers investors a commodity-FX trade: 
Chile and Peru sit on roughly a quarter of the world’s copper 
reserves.
10
 Brazil not only adds iron ore, agriculture and oil, but 
also already hosts 218 data centers, with the potential to double 
capacity by 2030.
11
 As investors in the region, we favor risk assets 
over currencies. We still favor Latin America, even after the Brazil 
election surprise, with a special preference for Mexico and a more 
neutral stance on Chile and Peru.

### Page 24

The case for gold and commodities 
In a fragmented world, we believe that gold and other commodities may be  
worth considering as additions to portfolios.
Turning first to gold, many countries have expressed a desire to hold savings in  
something outside the dollar-based system and broadly diversify away from fiat 
currencies.  As a result, central banks have increased their gold holdings as a share  
of overall reserves. The shift hasn’t been uniform, but IMF data shows the dollar’s 
share of official foreign exchange reserves has fallen to roughly 57% from a peak  
of 70% in 2000.
12
Commodities such as copper, oil and gas serve as vital components in compute supply 
chains and national resiliency initiatives. Electrification and grid enhancement help drive 
a projected 25% supply shortfall of copper and 40% shortfall of lithium by 2035.
13

We expect supply shortages to persist even as producers ramp up to meet growing 
demand. New copper mines, for example, take more than 16 years to build.
14
 Importantly, 
too, exposure to commodities offers a portfolio hedge to higher inflation. In fact, over  
the last 50 years commodities have delivered positive returns on average in quarters 
when stock and bond prices both decline.
Despite constrained supply and ongoing structural demand, the stocks of commodity  
and natural resource companies are generally less expensive than the overall market.  
The global energy and materials sectors trade at a 33% and 15% discount to the  
S&P 500, respectively, and sport higher dividend yields.

GLOBAL FRAGMENTATION

### Page 25

25
Do security spending beneficiaries
deserve your capital?
Global fragmentation has supported businesses that benefit from
security spending. The trend will continue, we believe. Indeed,
global fragmentation could be one of the best reasons to
invest globally in equity markets. Most countries face security
vulnerabilities they need to address and most markets host the
companies that benefit from the spending, from Japan and Korea
to France, Sweden and Mexico.

Generally, buyers of security-related products and services
care more about reliability and quick delivery than price. As
we’ve discussed, supply shortages of critical components like
transformers (with lead times between 2.5 and 3 years) and
copper will persist for the foreseeable future.
15
That gives security
companies pricing power and order books that run for years.
Strong earnings (seven of every 10 stocks we follow on this theme
have positive earnings revisions over the last 12 months) and

rising expectations have pushed a basket of security stocks to
a nearly 25% return year to date. We expect continued earnings
growth will drive the next leg of outperformance. Active stock
selection will be critical, though, in separating the market’s
winners and losers and identifying the firms that deserve
your capital.
Where we are placing
our capital
We are placing our own capital behind the need for security
spending in a fragmented world. JPMorganChase’s Security

and Resiliency Initiative (SRI) is a USD 1.5 trillion, 10-year
plan to facilitate, finance and invest in industries critical to
economic security and resiliency.
16
The firm will make direct
equity investments for an initial amount of USD 10 billion in select
companies, primarily in the United States but also in Europe,
to help enhance their growth, spur innovation and accelerate
strategic manufacturing. The remainder of the pledge is designed
to secure debt and loan financing for similar initiatives. Some

recent examples include a financing commitment to MP Materials,
a rare earth metals company, and an equity investment in Shield
AI, a leader in defense technology and autonomous aircraft.
We are also focused on the USD 1.3 trillion upgrade needed for
Europe’s electrical grid.
We are committed to doing our part to address the vulnerabilities
that are inherent in a fragmented world.

2027 ANNUAL OUTLOOK

Part 3

### Page 27

27
WILL THE AI CAPEX BOOM ULTIMATELY PAY OFF?27
Investors must consider four related issues:
• How far and how fast will AI adoption (early use of AI models)
and diffusion (more extensive use across the economy) progress?
• How many trillions will AI need to deliver to generate returns
for investors?
• Will markets be willing to finance the infrastructure
build-out, and at what price?
• Which layer of the equity stack will be best positioned to generate returns:

the infrastructure layer that builds the AI data centers,
the compute layer that rents out the semiconductors, or the
application layer that turns model tokens (chunks of text)
into durable business revenue?
The AI build-out has created a staggering
demand for capital. Going forward,
political pressure will increasingly
impact the capex boom.
Since 2022, AI capex has totaled over
USD 2 trillion, with trillions more
expected in the next several years.

Will the capex continue?
Yes, if investors like you fund it, and
regulations allow it. And, perhaps more
importantly for long-term investors:
Will AI spending ultimately pay off?
These may be the most important
questions for investors heading into 2027.

### Page 28

28
WILL THE AI CAPEX BOOM ULTIMATELY PAY OFF?
These four issues inform much of today’s market debate. If you
already own U.S. equities, you have been a passive participant.
That’s because, as our Chairman of Market and Investment
Strategy, Michael Cembalest, has shown, a basket of 54 AI stocks
represents over half of the S&P 500 market cap, 66% of earnings
growth, 59% of the index price return and 82% of capex/R&D
growth since January 2024.
17
We believe in AI’s long-term potential to boost productivity and

corporate profits. But investors are asking serious questions
about the magnitude of the infrastructure build, and markets
may be starting to shift their perception of who the ultimate
winners will be.
A stronger corporate investment cycle is driving higher borrowing costs

Our portfolio positioning reflects a reasonably optimistic outlook.
We believe investment grade AI-related debt, which offers
attractive all-in yields, is more secure than media headlines
suggest. Today, we think that it will be difficult for data center
infrastructure-related names (e.g. semiconductors) to exceed

expectations. Hyperscalers and privately-owned application
companies seem best positioned to provide strong returns to
your capital from here.

### Page 29

29
WILL THE AI CAPEX BOOM ULTIMATELY PAY OFF?29
If AI adoption and diffusion stall, nothing else really matters. The infrastructure  
gets stranded, the compute rents compress and the applications never find their  
potential customers. 
Data tells us that these are still very early days for AI adoption. According to Ramp,  
a financial technology company that tracks business spending, 80% of AI spending is 
driven by 1% of businesses, overwhelmingly technology startups buying tokens from 
other technology startups.
18
 In an analysis of AI and productivity, J.P. Morgan Asset 
Management estimates that only 6% of U.S. work hours are AI-assisted today, even  
as two-thirds of tasks may be theoretically exposed.
19

To justify the trillions of capital targeted for the AI build-out, adoption and diffusion must 
broaden beyond the technology sector and deepen beyond pilot programs. We think this 
will happen, for three reasons.
First, enterprise adoption is shifting from experimentation to productivity. Most large 
enterprises remain skittish about AI agents, which operate with minimal human 
involvement. But as corporate leaders grow more comfortable with the technology, 
we think they will increasingly deploy agentic models that will boost productivity and 
compute intensity per user.
Second, the history of transformative technologies tells us that falling prices unlock 
use cases faster than they compress revenue. Since 2023, the price of a given level 
of AI capability has fallen at a 13x annual rate, which is four times faster than DNA 
sequencing.
20
 Over the same period, annual run-rate revenues for the leading model 
providers have grown from zero to well over USD 100 billion.
21
 Goldman Sachs projects 
a 24x increase in token consumption through 2030 as use cases expand.
22
 J.P. Morgan 
Asset Management projects that overall AI adoption could rise to 40% of today’s tasks by 
2040.
23
Third, consumer agents, which can autonomously handle a range of tasks, could 
gain traction. Meta’s Muse has emerged as one contender in this arena. In many 
large economic sectors like healthcare and transportation, AI models still have a low 
penetration rate, and that too could change.
24

The bear case for AI adoption is straightforward: AI remains a productivity tool for 
technologists and a novelty for everyone else. The error rate remains too high and 
enterprise adoption stalls while most consumers decide to take a pass on the new 
AI spreads through  
the economy—or  
all bets are off
